In the Code section the line of a GitHub line fragment (`L<n>`, `L<n>-L<m>`, `L<n>C<c>`) is shown, as in the doc preview ([Link activation](documentation-viewer.md#link-activation)). Other links, a `#fragment` link included, do nothing. The same window choice applies. Default chosen at design time.
- Ticket and bundle ids: the transcript renders with `projectIdentifiers` set to the identifiers of every project of the window's device, sub projects included, so the ids of these projects are links ([Ticket and bundle ids](documentation-viewer.md#ticket-and-bundle-ids)); the pinned cards use the same identifiers. A click on an id, with or without ⌘, navigates to the `ticket` target, or to the `bundle` target for an id of the bundle form ([Resolving a target](ui.md#resolving-a-target)), with the same window choice: the chat view's main window, or from a chat window the main window chosen by [Choosing the window](ui.md#choosing-the-window). A ticket or bundle that does not exist shows `Ticket <id> was not found.` or `Bundle <id> was not found.`. In the transcript, id links look like its other links: the `.md a` style of [Styling](documentation-viewer.md#styling), white and underlined in `user` bubbles, and the same style outside Markdown text (`a.md-id` in `chat.css`). The same rules apply to a chat view shown in a ticket's or a bundle's Chats tab.
- A link click that fails, such as a failed `webTabs.open`, shows the alert `Open Link failed`; a file of the transcript that cannot be read shows the alert `Open failed` ([Principles](ui.md#principles)). Default chosen at design time.
- Images use URLs `hephaestus-attachment://attachment/<percent-encoded path relative to .hephaestus/>`, served by a `WKURLSchemeHandler` that calls `attachments.read` for the chat's project through the window's engine connection; results are cached per window.
- Clicking an image opens the [image viewer](tickets.md#image-viewer). Clicking another file opens it with Quick Look on a temporary copy, as for ticket attachments ([Tickets](tickets.md#showing-attachments)).
- An SF Symbol marker is a Markdown image whose source has the scheme `sf`, for example `![](sf:line.3.horizontal.decrease.circle)` ([SF Symbol markers](documentation-viewer.md#sf-symbol-markers)). The transcript renders Markdown with `isSymbolAvailable` true for a name when `NSImage(systemSymbolName:accessibilityDescription:)` gives an image for it on the Mac that shows the window. A marker of such a name is drawn as the symbol's icon followed by its name; any other marker shows its text unchanged, for example `![](sf:no.such.symbol)`.
- The icon is the `md-symbol-icon` element of the marker: 1.25em wide and 1em high, 0.25em before the name, filled with the color of the text around it through a mask that holds the symbol's image, scaled to fit and centered. `chat.js` sets that mask to `hephaestus-symbol://symbol/<name>`, the name encoded with `encodeURIComponent`, and the page's Content Security Policy allows `hephaestus-symbol:` images. Default chosen at design time.
- `SymbolSchemeHandler`, registered for `hephaestus-symbol` on the transcript web view only, answers a GET whose host is `symbol` with a PNG of the symbol named by the percent-decoded path without its leading `/`, drawn from `NSImage(systemSymbolName:accessibilityDescription:)` at 48 points and scale 2. The response has status 200, `Content-Type: image/png`, `Content-Length` and `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`, since WebKit loads mask images in CORS mode. It makes no engine call, so a window showing a remote device draws the symbols of its own Mac. A name without symbol fails with `URLError(.fileDoesNotExist)` and a malformed URL with `URLError(.noPermissionsToReadFile)`. Default chosen at design time.
- A color marker is a Markdown image whose source has the scheme `color`, for example `![](color:#FF9500)` or `![](color:systemBlue)` ([Color markers](documentation-viewer.md#color-markers)). The transcript renders Markdown with `colorSwatch`, which accepts a hex color `#RRGGBB`, in either letter case, or a name of [System colors](#system-colors), and gives the color's light and dark values on the Mac that shows the window. A marker of such a color is drawn as a swatch of the color followed by the color as written; any other marker shows its text unchanged, for example `![](color:#FF950080)`.
- The swatch is the `md-color-swatch` element of the marker: a 0.9em square with corners of radius 0.2em and a 1px border of the text color at 30 % opacity, 0.25em before the color, aligned with the text. Its background is the element's `data-light` value, or its `data-dark` value while the page is in the dark appearance (`prefers-color-scheme: dark`); `chat.js` copies them into the CSS variables `--md-color-light` and `--md-color-dark` of the element. A hex color has its own value in both; a system color has the values the Mac that shows the window gives it in the light (Aqua) and dark (Dark Aqua) appearances ([System colors](#system-colors)), written `#RRGGBB`, or `#RRGGBBAA` when it is not opaque. Default chosen at design time.
- A sound marker is a Markdown image whose source has the scheme `sound`, for example `![](sound:macOS/Glass.aiff)` ([Sound markers](documentation-viewer.md#sound-markers)). The name, a name of the [sound catalogue](#sound-catalogue), is written as it is, spaces included: `![](sound:iOS 26.1/sms-received1.caf)`. The marker ends at the `)` that closes its `(`, so a name may also hold balanced parentheses, as in `![](sound:Project/Sounds/alert (2).caf)`; the CommonMark form with angle brackets, `![](<sound:iOS 26.1/sms-received1.caf>)`, is read too. The transcript renders Markdown with `readsSoundMarkers` true and draws every marker as a play button followed by the name; the name is not checked against the catalogue, so the play button of a sound that does not exist fails when clicked ([Sound playback](#sound-playback)).
- The play button is the `md-sound-play` button of the marker, which holds the `play.circle.fill` icon, drawn as the icon of an SF Symbol marker (`md-symbol-icon`); the name follows it. A click on the button posts `playSound` with the name ([Web view and bridge](#web-view-and-bridge)), and Swift plays the sound of the chat's project ([Sound playback](#sound-playback)). Default chosen at design time.
- In the question texts of the pinned cards, which SwiftUI draws, the play button of a sound marker is the character `▶` drawn as a link, followed by a space and the name; a click on it plays the sound. The option rows of the pinned questions card show a sound marker as its text, so that a click in a row always chooses the option. Default chosen at design time.
- A shortcut marker is a Markdown image whose source has the scheme `shortcut`, for example `![](shortcut:⇧⌘K)` ([Shortcut markers](documentation-viewer.md#shortcut-markers)). The transcript renders Markdown with `shortcutKeys` giving the key caps of a shortcut written in the [shortcut notation](#shortcut-notation), and nothing for any other text. A marker of such a shortcut is drawn as its key caps, inline with the text: each key cap is a `kbd` element, a rounded box with a thin border in the color of the text around it, 0.15em apart; any other marker shows its text unchanged, for example `![](shortcut:⌘⇧K)`. Default chosen at design time for the drawing.
- In a shortcut marker, a key `(`, `)` or `\` is written with a `\` before it, as Markdown requires in a link destination: `![](shortcut:⌘\()` names the shortcut `⌘(`. The pinned shortcut card writes its answers this way. Default chosen at design time.
- A font marker is a Markdown image whose source has the scheme `font` followed by a font of the [font notation](#font-notation), for example `![](font:.headline)` or `![](font:System Rounded/Semibold/17)` ([Font markers](documentation-viewer.md#font-markers)). A family or weight name with spaces is written with its spaces, `![](font:Avenir Next/Demi Bold/15)`: CommonMark takes no space in an image source, so the renderers find such a marker in the text around it.
- The transcript renders Markdown with a `fontMarker` that accepts every font that follows the [font notation](#font-notation). A marker of such a font is drawn as the rows of the pinned font card read it, `System Rounded · Semibold · 17 pt` or `.headline`, at the size of the text around it: a family font in its family and weight, a text style in the system font with the weight of its macOS style. Any other marker shows its text unchanged, for example `![](font:Helvetica)`.
- The marker is the `md-font` element of [Font markers](documentation-viewer.md#font-markers). For a system design, `chat.js` sets its CSS `font-family` to `system-ui`, `ui-rounded`, `ui-serif` or `ui-monospace` and its `font-weight` to 100 for `Ultralight` up to 900 for `Black`, in the order of the nine weights; for a text style, `system-ui` with the weight 700 for `.headline`, 500 for `.caption2` and 400 for the others. For another family, the marker's face URL is `hephaestus-font://face/<family>/<weight>`, each name percent-encoded as `encodeURIComponent` does; `chat.js` adds that face to the page's fonts (`FontFace`) the first time a marker uses it, and draws the marker in it, or in `system-ui` while it is not loaded or when it does not load. The page's Content Security Policy allows `hephaestus-font:` fonts. Default chosen at design time.
- `FontSchemeHandler`, registered for `hephaestus-font` on the transcript web view only, answers a GET whose host is `face` with the font of the face named by the two percent-decoded components of the path: a face of a family installed on the Mac that shows the window, else a face of a project family of the chat's project, read with `files.fonts` and `files.read` through the window's engine connection ([Font catalogue](#font-catalogue)). The data is one OpenType font rebuilt from the face's tables, so a face of a collection loads too. The response has status 200, `Content-Type: font/otf` for a font with CFF outlines and `font/ttf` otherwise, `Content-Length` and `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`. A face that does not exist fails with `URLError(.fileDoesNotExist)` and a malformed URL with `URLError(.noPermissionsToReadFile)`. Default chosen at design time.
- An animation marker is a Markdown image whose source has the scheme `animation`, for example `![](animation:.spring(duration: 0.4, bounce: 0.2))` ([Animation markers](documentation-viewer.md#animation-markers)). The expression follows `animation:` as written, its spaces and parentheses included; as a CommonMark image source cannot hold spaces, the renderers find the marker in the text, from `![` to the `)` that closes the expression's own parentheses. The transcript renders Markdown with `animationCurve`, which gives, for an expression of [Animation expressions](workflow.md#animation-expressions), the duration and the easing of its animation as SwiftUI plays it. A marker of such an expression is drawn as a replay button with the symbol `play.fill`, then a small lane, then the expression; any other marker shows its text unchanged, for example `![](animation:.wobbly)`. The pinned cards show animation markers as their text.
- The marker is the `md-animation` element: the button `md-animation-play`, which holds the `md-symbol-icon` of `play.fill` drawn as the icon of an SF Symbol marker, the lane `md-animation-lane`, a track 3em wide and 0.8em high with round ends filled with the color of the text around it at 20% opacity, which holds the circle `md-animation-dot`, 0.8em across in the color of the text, and the expression. A click on the button plays the marker's [lane](#lanes): `chat.js` cancels the circle's running animation, then animates it with the Web Animations API from the left end to the right end of the track, for the marker's `data-duration` seconds with the CSS easing of its `data-easing`, and leaves it at the right end. The click does nothing else. Default chosen at design time.
- `animationCurve` samples the SwiftUI animation at 61 times evenly spaced from 0 to its duration: for `.smooth`, `.snappy`, `.bouncy` and `.spring`, the duration is the settling duration of SwiftUI's `Spring` with the same parameters, at most 10 s, and 10 s for a spring that never settles, and the values are that spring's values for a move from 0 to 1, a value SwiftUI cannot compute counting as 1; for `.easeIn`, `.easeOut`, `.easeInOut` and `.linear`, the duration is the expression's and the values those of SwiftUI's `UnitCurve` of the same name. `data-duration` is the duration in seconds with three decimals, and `data-easing` the CSS function `linear(<value>, …)` with each value rounded to four decimals, so a spring that passes the right end draws the circle past it. Default chosen at design time.

### Selection and copying

All text of the transcript is selectable across bubbles. ⌘A selects the whole transcript and ⌘C copies the selection as plain text. Code blocks have a Copy button; bubbles have a Copy button on hover ([Item rendering](#item-rendering)). Find (⌘F) shows the find bar of the Docs preview above the transcript ([Modes](documentation-viewer.md#modes)).

### Scrolling and long transcripts

- When the view is scrolled within 40 pt of the bottom, new content keeps it at the bottom; otherwise a `Jump to Latest` button appears at the bottom edge.
- A transcript renders its last 300 items; the `Show Earlier Messages` button at the top adds the 300 previous ones each time. Default chosen at design time.
- The transcript page scrolls vertically only, in every chat view: `chat.css` hides the page's horizontal overflow (`overflow-x: hidden` on `html`) and keeps the bubbles and cards within the page width, wrapping long words. Wide code blocks and tables scroll horizontally inside their own box ([Code blocks](documentation-viewer.md#code-blocks), [Tables](documentation-viewer.md#tables)). `markdown.css` is unchanged, so the Docs preview keeps its own scrolling.

### Empty transcript

Placeholders: Default chosen at design time.

| Kind     | Placeholder                                                   |
| -------- | ------------------------------------------------------------- |
| project  | Ask Claude to plan, create tickets or work on <project name>. |
| ticket   | Ask Claude about <ticket id>, or click Implement.             |
| bundle   | Ask Claude about <bundle id>, or click Implement All.         |
| feedback | never empty: the feedback message is sent at creation         |

## Agents

A chat's Claude can start agents that work beside it: the agents of a workflow it runs with the Workflow tool, which [ultracode](#ultracode) makes it use for most of its work, and the sub-agents it starts with the Agent tool ([Sub-agents](#sub-agents)). The engine records the workflows and agents of each chat, follows them live, keeps them as long as the chat exists, and gives every client, remote devices included, their states and transcripts ([Engine API](engine-api.md#chats)). The user sees them as rows under their chat in the chat lists ([Where chats appear](#where-chats-appear)), reads each agent's transcript in its [agent view](#agent-view), and stops a running workflow with all its agents ([Stopping a workflow](#stopping-a-workflow)). The rules below are checked against `stream-workflow.jsonl`, `stream-workflow-stop.jsonl` and `stream-background-agent.jsonl` ([Fixture replays](#fixture-replays)).

### Workflows and agents

| Record         | Created when                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | `id`                   | Title                                                                  |
| -------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| workflow       | a `system/task_started` line with `task_type` `local_workflow` whose `task_id` names no workflow of the chat                                                                                                                           | the line's `task_id`   | its `summary`: the line's `description`, else `Workflow`               |
| workflow agent | a `workflow_agent` entry with an `agentId` string in the `workflow_progress` of a `system/task_progress` line of the workflow, or a `started` entry with an `agentId` string in the journal of its [workflow folder](#workflow-folder) | the `agentId`          | the entry's `label`, else `Agent`                                      |
| sub-agent      | a `toolUse` item named `Agent` or `Task` whose `server` is null ([Sub-agents](#sub-agents))                                                                                                                                            | the call's `toolUseID` | the call's input `description`, else its `subagent_type`, else `Agent` |

- A workflow is a [ChatWorkflow](data-model.md#chatworkflow): its id, the `toolUseID` of its `Workflow` call (the line's `tool_use_id`), its `runID` ([Workflow folder](#workflow-folder)), its `name` (the line's `workflow_name`), its `summary`, its state, and its `started` and `ended` dates.
- An agent is a [ChatAgent](data-model.md#chatagent): its id, its kind (`workflow` for a workflow agent, `subagent` for a sub-agent), the `workflowID` of its workflow, its title, its `phase` (the entry's `phaseTitle`, or the journal entry's `phase`; none for a sub-agent), its state, and its `started` and `ended` dates.
- A `task_progress` line of a workflow whose `summary` string differs from the workflow's replaces it. A later `workflow_agent` entry of a known agent replaces its title and phase with its `label` and `phaseTitle` strings.
- `started` is the time the engine created the record and `ended` the time the record ended, in UTC with seconds.
- `system/task_started` lines of other task types (`local_bash`, `local_agent`, others) create no record: a sub-agent comes from its `Agent` call, which the engine reads first.
- The titles of agents, the label of a workflow agent and the description of a sub-agent's call, and the fallback titles: Default chosen at design time.

### Agent states

`ChatAgentState` is the state of a workflow or an agent:

| State     | Meaning                                                             | Mark in the chat lists |
| --------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------- |
| running   | It works.                                                           | a spinner              |
| completed | It ended with its result.                                           | none                   |
| failed    | It ended with an error.                                             | a red dot              |
| stopped   | It was stopped, or its chat's Claude process ended while it worked. | none                   |

The marks are those of chat rows for the states running, idle and error ([Where chats appear](#where-chats-appear)). A record is created running. Only a running record changes; one that has ended keeps its state:

| Record         | Event                                                                                                          | New state                             |
| -------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------- |
| workflow       | `system/task_updated` of the workflow with `patch.status` `completed`, `failed` or `killed`                    | completed, failed or stopped          |
| workflow       | `system/task_notification` of the workflow with `status` `completed`, `failed` or `stopped`                    | completed, failed or stopped          |
| workflow agent | its `workflow_agent` entry has the `state` `done` or `error`                                                   | completed or failed                   |
| workflow agent | a `result` entry with its `agentId` in the journal of the workflow folder                                      | completed                             |
| workflow agent | its workflow has ended and the last read of the folder did not end it                                          | stopped                               |
| sub-agent      | a `system/task_notification` whose `tool_use_id` is its call, with `status` `completed`, `failed` or `stopped` | completed, failed or stopped          |
| sub-agent      | the `toolResult` item of its call, when the call's input has no `run_in_background` true                       | failed when `isError`, else completed |
| sub-agent      | a `result` line, when the call's input has no `run_in_background` true                                         | stopped                               |
| any            | the chat's Claude process exits                                                                                | stopped                               |
| any            | the engine reads it from the chat's agents file while it is running there                                      | stopped                               |

- The `workflow_agent` states `start` and `progress` leave an agent running. A sub-agent started in the background (`run_in_background` true) gets its result through `task_notification`; the `toolResult` of its call only says that it was launched ([Background work](#background-work)).
- The last row covers an engine that stopped, with the app, while a workflow or an agent worked: its chat's process ended with it. The engine does not rewrite the file for it.

### Workflow folder

The result of the `Workflow` call that launched a workflow ([Tool results](#tool-results)) is a text whose lines include:

```
Workflow launched in background. Task ID: wflx39ys6
Summary: Two probe agents
Transcript dir: /Users/example/.claude/projects/-tmp-example/1EADA864-B5CE-4D35-9071-C2FBF075F979/subagents/workflows/wf_0afd7514-9e5
Run ID: wf_0afd7514-9e5
```

The engine takes the workflow whose id follows `Task ID: `, sets its `runID` to the text after `Run ID: ` and follows the folder named after `Transcript dir: `, when that is an absolute path whose last component is the run id. A result without these lines changes nothing; a result whose task id names no workflow of the chat yet is applied when the `task_started` line of that task arrives. The CLI writes in that folder:

| File                        | Content                                                                                                                                                                        | Read by the engine                                                                                  |
| --------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `journal.jsonl`             | one JSON object per line: `launched`, a `started` entry with `agentId`, `label` and `phase` for each agent that starts, and a `result` entry with `agentId` for each that ends | `started` entries create workflow agents, `result` entries complete them; other entries are ignored |
| `agent-<agentId>.jsonl`     | the agent's transcript, one JSON object per line                                                                                                                               | mapped to items ([Agent transcript lines](#agent-transcript-lines)), for each agent of the workflow |
| `agent-<agentId>.meta.json` | the agent's `description` and `workflowPhase`                                                                                                                                  | not read                                                                                            |

- The engine reads the folder when the tool result arrives, then every second while the workflow runs, and a last time once it has ended. Each read takes the complete lines added to each file since the previous read. The interval: Default chosen at design time.
- A file that does not exist or cannot be read yet is read again at the next read; a line that is not valid JSON is skipped.
- The engine only reads these files; it never writes, moves or deletes them. What it read stays in the chat's own files ([Storage of agents](#storage-of-agents)), so the agents stay viewable after the CLI removes its session files.

### Agent transcript lines

Each line of `agent-<agentId>.jsonl` becomes items of the agent's transcript, of the item types of the chat transcript ([Data model](data-model.md#chat-items)):

| Line                                                                      | Items                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `assistant`                                                               | one item per block of `message.content`, mapped as [Assistant blocks](#assistant-blocks) map them: `assistant` for `text`, `thinking` for a `thinking` block whose text is not empty, `toolUse` for `tool_use`, with `parentToolUseID` null; id `<message.id>#<n>`, `<n>` being the block's index in `message.content` plus the line's integer `apiBlockIndex`, 0 when the line has none |
| `user` whose `message.content` is a string and whose `isMeta` is not true | one `user` item with that text, `sender` `Claude` and no attachment; id the line's `uuid`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| `user` whose `message.content` is an array                                | one `toolResult` item per `tool_result` block, mapped as [Tool results](#tool-results) map them, without images; and when it has `text` blocks, one `user` item with their texts joined with `\n`, `sender` `Claude`, id the line's `uuid`                                                                                                                                               |
| any other line (`attachment`, `api-request`, others)                      | none                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |

- `created` is the line's `timestamp` truncated to seconds, else the time the engine read the line.
- The [size limits](#size-limits) apply. An item whose id the agent's transcript already holds is not added again.
- The sender `Claude` of the agent's task and the images left out: Default chosen at design time.

### Sub-agent transcripts

The transcript of a sub-agent is made of the chat's own items, which hold its work ([Sub-agents](#sub-agents)):

1. A `user` item with the call's input `prompt` (empty when it has none), `sender` `Claude`, id `<toolUseID>#prompt` and the `created` of the call's `toolUse` item.
2. Then, in transcript order, every `assistant`, `thinking` and `toolUse` item whose `parentToolUseID` is the call's `toolUseID`, or the `toolUseID` of an `Agent` or `Task` call among these items at any depth, and the `toolResult` item of each of these `toolUse` items.

When the engine creates a sub-agent, and when it adds to the chat's transcript an item of a sub-agent's transcript, it emits `chat.agentItem` with that item for the sub-agent, and for each sub-agent whose transcript holds the item. These transcripts are not stored a second time. Default chosen at design time.

### Storage of agents

- Each new record, and each change of a record, appends the whole record to the chat's agents file `chats/<chat id>/agents.jsonl` ([Data model](data-model.md#chat-agents-file)), then the engine emits `chat.agents` with the chat's complete workflows and agents.
- Each item of a workflow agent's transcript is appended to the file `chats/<chat id>/agents/<agent id>.jsonl` ([Data model](data-model.md#agent-transcript-file)) and emitted as `chat.agentItem` {chatID, agentID, item}.
- The engine reads a chat's agents file the first time it needs the chat's records: for `chats.listAgents` of its project, for `chats.agentTranscript`, or when the chat's process reports a workflow or a sub-agent.
- The records of a closed chat stay; the chat lists hide them with their chat. `chats.delete` moves the chat's folder `chats/<chat id>/`, these files with it, to the Trash ([Persistence](#persistence)).

### Stopping a workflow

Stop Workflow in the context menu of a workflow row or of one of its agent rows, the Stop button of its [workflow view](#workflow-view) and the Stop button of the [agent view](#agent-view) of one of its agents call `chats.stopWorkflow` ([Engine API](engine-api.md#chatsstopworkflow)) while the workflow runs. The engine writes the control request `stop_task` with the workflow's id to the chat's live process ([Control protocol](#control-protocol)), in every chat state, and remembers that it stopped that task. Claude Code 2.1.309 stops the workflow and its agents and reports it with these lines, checked against `stream-workflow-stop.jsonl`:

| Line                                                           | Engine reaction                                                                    |
| -------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `system/task_updated` of the workflow, `patch.status` `killed` | the workflow and its running agents become stopped ([Agent states](#agent-states)) |
| `system/task_notification` of the workflow, `status` `stopped` | no notification turn becomes pending ([Background work](#background-work))         |
| `system/background_tasks_changed` without the workflow         | the process's background work ends when nothing else runs                          |
| `control_response` success for the request, `response` `{}`    | nothing                                                                            |

- The CLI starts no CLI turn for a workflow stopped this way: Claude learns that it was stopped with its next message.
- An error answer, or no answer within 30 s, adds the system item `The workflow “<summary>” was not stopped: <description>.` with level warning, `<description>` being the CLI's error or `no answer`; the workflow keeps running. Default chosen at design time.
- Sub-agents have no Stop control. Stop in the composer stops the turn as [Stopping a turn](#stopping-a-turn) describes; a sub-agent then ends as [Agent states](#agent-states) describes.

### Agent view

Selecting an agent row ([Where chats appear](#where-chats-appear)) shows the agent view in place of the chat view: beside the list in the Chats section, under the list in the Chats tab of a ticket or a bundle.

- The header shows the agent's title, a chip with `Agent`, followed by ` · <phase>` for an agent with a phase, and the agent's state mark. For an agent of a running workflow, a `Stop` button with the SF Symbol `stop.fill` at its trailing edge stops the whole workflow ([Stopping a workflow](#stopping-a-workflow)); a sub-agent's view has none.
- Under the header, the agent's transcript, loaded with `chats.agentTranscript` and kept current by `chat.agentItem` ([UI state](#ui-state)), in the transcript web view of [Transcript view](#transcript-view): the same [item rendering](#item-rendering), [tool cards](#tool-cards), links, find and copying. A tool card without result shows its spinner while the agent runs.
- The view is read-only: it has no composer, no status line and no pinned cards.
- Layout, texts and symbol: Default chosen at design time.

### Workflow view

Selecting a workflow row shows the workflow view in the same place:

- The header shows the workflow's summary, a chip with `Workflow`, followed by ` · <name>` when the workflow has a name, the workflow's state mark and, while it runs, a `Stop` button with the SF Symbol `stop.fill` that stops it ([Stopping a workflow](#stopping-a-workflow)).
- Under the header, one row per agent of the workflow, in the order of their `started`: the agent's state mark, its title, its phase in secondary color, and at the trailing edge its state, `Running`, `Completed`, `Failed` or `Stopped`. A click on a row selects that agent's row and shows its agent view. A workflow without agent yet shows `No agents yet`.
- Layout and texts: Default chosen at design time.

## Chat views and windows

### Where chats appear

| Place                    | Content                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| ------------------------ | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Inspector (⌥⌘0)          | The project chat panel: a picker of the project's chats of kind project, New Chat, and the selected chat's view ([UI](ui.md#inspector)).                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| Chats section (⌘4)       | The project's chats grouped by kind in the order Project, Ticket, Bundle, Feedback, each group titled `Project`, `Ticket`, `Bundle` or `Feedback`, as the kind chip names the kind, and listed newest `updated` first; beside the list, the selected chat's view, or `<n> Chats Selected` and a Close button while several chats are selected. |
| Ticket detail, Chats tab | The ticket's chats, Implement and New Chat ([Tickets](tickets.md#ticket-chats-and-implement)).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| Bundle detail, Chats tab | The bundle's chats, each followed by the ticket chats it orchestrates, Implement All and New Chat ([Tickets](tickets.md#implement-all)).                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| Chat window              | One chat in its own window, opened by Open in New Window ([UI](ui.md#windows)).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |

- A row of the Chats section shows the title, the linked ticket or bundle id, a state mark (none for idle, a spinner for starting and running, an orange dot for waitingPermission and waitingAnswers, a red dot for error) and the relative `updated` time. The list header has a New Chat button (`plus.bubble`); with no chat, the section shows `No chats yet` and the same button. It shows a draft project chat in the section's chat view with the focus in its composer; its first Send creates the chat, which is listed in the Project group and becomes the only selected chat (`selectedChatIDs`). Default chosen at design time.
- Selection: click, ⌘-click and ⇧-click; ⌘A selects every visible chat, as in the [Ticket list](tickets.md#ticket-list). With one chat selected, its view shows beside the list; with several, the section shows `<n> Chats Selected`, `<n>` being the number of selected chats, and a Close button, which closes them ([Closing a chat](#closing-a-chat)); with none, the area beside the list is empty. The selection is `ProjectViewState.selectedChatIDs` ([WindowState](ui.md#windowstate)). When a selected chat is deleted, the section removes it from its selection. The Chats tabs of tickets and bundles keep a single selected chat ([Ticket chats and Implement](tickets.md#ticket-chats-and-implement)).
- The context menu of the chat rows of the Chats section acts on the selected chats; right-clicking a chat row that is not selected selects it first. Its items are Open in New Window, Rename…, Close and Delete…. With one chat selected every item is enabled, except Close while that chat's `closed` is true; with several, Close is the only enabled item, enabled when at least one of them has `closed` false ([Closing a chat](#closing-a-chat)), and Open in New Window, Rename… and Delete… are disabled. Rename… edits the row's title in place, as the chat view's title. ⌫ and ⌘⌫ act as Delete… while one chat is selected ([Menus and keyboard shortcuts](ui.md#menus-and-keyboard-shortcuts)). The place of Close in the menu: Default chosen at design time.
- A chat is closed while its `closed` is true ([Closing a chat](#closing-a-chat)), or while its linked ticket, the `ticket` of a ticket chat or of a feedback chat linked to the ticket it created, has the status `done` ([Status rules](tickets.md#status-rules)). It is open again once it is reopened, or a message delivered to it sets `closed` to false, and its ticket is no longer `done`, for example after the user sets it back to To Do or In Progress. Each client computes it from the chats and tickets it holds, so the lists change as `chat.updated` and `ticket.updated` arrive.
- The list header of the Chats section has a filter button with the symbol `line.3.horizontal.decrease.circle`, left of New Chat, that hides or shows the closed chats. Its symbol is filled (`line.3.horizontal.decrease.circle.fill`) while closed chats are hidden. Its tooltip is `Show Closed Chats` while closed chats are hidden and `Hide Closed Chats` while they are shown. The place of the button: Default chosen at design time.
- Closed chats are hidden by default. The filter state is `ProjectViewState.hidesClosedChats` ([WindowState](ui.md#windowstate)): one value per window and project, shared by the Chats section and the Chats tabs of tickets and bundles ([Ticket chats and Implement](tickets.md#ticket-chats-and-implement)), so a click on the button in any of these lists changes all of them. It is persisted with the window and restored after a relaunch, for every project the window showed.
- When selected chats become hidden, because the filter is turned on or the chats become closed, for example after Close, their rows disappear and the area beside the list, the chat's view or `<n> Chats Selected`, stays until another chat is selected. A selection made in the list holds only the chats it lists.
- While the filter hides every chat of the project, the list shows `No open chats` and a New Chat button under its header, which keeps the filter button, and a group with no chat left is not shown. The inspector's picker lists closed chats too.
- The Chats section's list, its header included, has the same background as the [Ticket list](tickets.md#ticket-list) and the list of the [Bundles section](tickets.md#bundles-section): the system control background colour (`NSColor.controlBackgroundColor`), in light and dark appearance.
- Under each chat row of the Chats section and of the Chats tabs of tickets and bundles, the ticket chats under their bundle chat included, come the rows of the chat's workflows and sub-agents ([Agents](#agents)), in the order of their `started`: a workflow row titled with the workflow's summary, indented one step, followed by the rows of its agents, indented two steps, in the order of their `started`; and a row for each sub-agent, indented one step. A row shows an SF Symbol, `point.3.connected.trianglepath.dotted` for a workflow and `person.crop.circle` for an agent, its title, the phase of a workflow agent in secondary color, and the mark of its state ([Agent states](#agent-states)). Symbols and indentation: Default chosen at design time.
- A running workflow, with every agent row of it, and a running sub-agent are always shown. Once a workflow or a sub-agent has ended, its rows fold under the chat row, which then shows a disclosure triangle at its leading edge: pointing right while the ended rows are folded, down while they are shown; a click on it shows or folds them. They start folded. The unfolded chats are kept per window and per project while the window is open, shared by the Chats section and the Chats tabs (`expandedAgentChats` of [WindowState](ui.md#windowstate)). Default chosen at design time.
- A click on a workflow row shows its [workflow view](#workflow-view), and on an agent row its [agent view](#agent-view), in place of the chat view: beside the list in the Chats section, under the list in a Chats tab (`selectedChatAgent` and `chatsTabAgentSelection` of [WindowState](ui.md#windowstate)). Selecting a chat row shows its chat view again, and so does a navigation to a chat. In the Chats section a workflow or agent row is selected alone, and its chat becomes the only selected chat (`selectedChatIDs`): a selection of several rows, made with ⌘-click, ⇧-click or ⌘A, holds only the chats among them.
- While a workflow runs, the context menu of its workflow row and of each of its agent rows has the item Stop Workflow ([Stopping a workflow](#stopping-a-workflow)); a failed call shows the alert `Stop Workflow failed` ([Principles](ui.md#principles)). These rows have no other item, sub-agent rows have no context menu, and right-clicking these rows does not select them.
- The rows of a chat hidden by the closed-chats filter are hidden with it. When the chat of the shown workflow or agent is deleted, the list clears its selection, as for a deleted chat.
- Closing a chat window does not stop Claude. Window size, title, subtitle and restoration are in [UI](ui.md#windows).

### Draft chats

New Chat (⌥⌘N, or a New Chat button) shows a draft: an empty chat view whose composer uses the default settings ([Fields and defaults](#fields-and-defaults)) and has the keyboard focus. Nothing is stored until the first Send, which calls `chats.create` with the message and the draft's settings. A draft that is closed or replaced is discarded. Default chosen at design time.

### Chat view

From top to bottom: the header, the transcript, the pinned permission card, the pinned questions card, the pinned symbol card, the pinned color card, the pinned sound card, the pinned shortcut card, the pinned font card or the pinned animation card, the status line ([Chat states](#chat-states)), the composer. The pinned cards take at most half of the view's height. They scroll inside that space only when they are taller than it; while they fit, they neither scroll nor bounce under a scroll gesture. In both cases, wide code blocks and tables inside the cards scroll horizontally inside their own box.

The chat view's background is the window background colour, except in the inspector, where it draws none and shows the inspector's frosted material, with a transparent transcript ([Web view and bridge](#web-view-and-bridge)).

The [agent view](#agent-view) and the [workflow view](#workflow-view) take the place of the chat view while an agent row or a workflow row is selected ([Where chats appear](#where-chats-appear)); they have the chat view's background.

Header (in the Chats section, the ticket and bundle Chats tabs and chat windows; the inspector shows its picker instead):

- The title. Double-click edits it in place: Return calls `chats.rename`, Escape and leaving the field cancel. Default chosen at design time.
- A chip with the kind and, for ticket, bundle and linked feedback chats, the ticket or bundle id; clicking the id navigates to it (`ticket` or `bundle` target), with the same window choice as [Links, images and files](#links-images-and-files).
- Open in New Window (hidden inside a chat window) and a More menu with Rename… and Delete…, preceded by Close while the chat's `closed` is false and by Reopen while it is true. The header of a draft shows `New Chat` and the kind chip only, since nothing is stored yet. Default chosen at design time.
- Close asks `Close the chat “<title>”?` with the buttons Close and Cancel, then closes the chat ([Closing a chat](#closing-a-chat)); `This conversation is over` replaces the composer in every view of the chat. A failed call shows the alert `Close failed` ([Principles](ui.md#principles)).
- Reopen calls `chats.reopen` ([Closing a chat](#closing-a-chat)); the composer shows again in every view of the chat. A failed call shows the alert `Reopen failed` ([Principles](ui.md#principles)). The inspector shows its picker instead of the header, so a project chat is closed, and a closed project chat reopened, from a chat window or the Chats section.
- Delete… asks `Delete the chat “<title>”?` with the informative text `Its transcript and attachments are moved to the Trash.` and the buttons Delete (destructive) and Cancel.

### UI state

`DeviceWorkspace.transcripts` keeps one `TranscriptState` per chat shown in a view of that device ([UI](ui.md#deviceworkspace)): the items in transcript order and the streaming buffers. Pending permission requests, the incomplete question set, the symbol request without answer, the color request without answer, the sound request without answer, the shortcut request without answer, the font request without answer and the animation request without answer are read from the items. Every view of the chat in windows of the same device shares this state. Opening a chat calls `chats.get`; the events received while it is in flight are applied after its result, in arrival order ([UI](ui.md#deviceworkspace)).

`DeviceWorkspace.agents` keeps the workflows and agents of every chat of the device, by chat id: the workspace loads them with `chats.listAgents` for every project, after its `chats.list`, and keeps them current with `chat.agents`. `DeviceWorkspace.agentTranscripts` keeps one `TranscriptState` per agent shown in a view, by `<chat id>/<agent id>`: opening the agent view calls `chats.agentTranscript`, `chat.agentItem` adds or replaces its items, and the state is dropped when the last view of the agent closes. Both follow the replay rule ([Seeding calls and the replay rule](remote.md#seeding-calls-and-the-replay-rule)).

## Implement and feedback chats

| Method                  | Chat created                                                                                                                                                                                                     | First message (`sender`)                                                                                                                                                     |
| ----------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `chats.implementTicket` | kind ticket, `ticket` = the ticket id, `questionsBlocked` true; the chat id is appended to the ticket's `chats`; `orchestrator` = the calling chat for an [orchestrated ticket chat](#orchestrated-ticket-chats) | the rendered prompt implement-ticket, or implement-bundle-ticket for an orchestrated ticket chat (the name of the user's device; `Claude` when called by `implement_ticket`) |
| `chats.implementBundle` | kind bundle, `bundle` = the bundle id; the chat id is appended to the bundle's `chats`                                                                                                                           | the rendered prompt implement-bundle (the name of the user's device; `Claude` when called by `implement_bundle`)                                                             |
| `chats.createFeedback`  | kind feedback, `ticket` null                                                                                                                                                                                     | the [feedback message](#feedback-message), with the screenshot as its only attachment (the name of the device that sent the feedback)                                        |

- A done ticket, and a bundle without a ticket left to implement, are refused with `conflict` ([Tickets](tickets.md#errors)).
- The chat that `chats.implementTicket` creates is an implement chat ([Claude workflow](workflow.md#implement-chats)): until the user writes in it, Claude asks no question and makes the choices itself. Its metadata holds `questionsBlocked: true` ([Data model](data-model.md#chat-metadata-file)), and while the key is set `chats.postQuestions`, `chats.postSymbol`, `chats.postColor`, `chats.postSound`, `chats.postShortcut`, `chats.postFont` and `chats.postAnimation` fail with `forbidden` and `This chat implements a ticket and cannot ask questions: decide yourself and note the choice in the completion comment.`, so `ask_questions` and the other ask tools show nothing to the user ([Posting questions](#posting-questions)).
- The first message the user sends in an implement chat with `chats.send`, from any device, removes the key, in the metadata write that follows its `user` item; the implement message does not. From then on Claude asks as in the other chats. A ticket chat created by `chats.create`, with New Chat, never has the key ([Engine API](engine-api.md#chatssend)).
- The new chat's settings are the `settings` param: the settings chosen in the [Implement sheet](tickets.md#implement-sheet) for Implement and Implement All, those of the feedback sheet's [chat settings menus](feedback.md#chat-settings-menus) for feedback. `implement_ticket` and `implement_bundle` send none, so the chat takes the project's `chatDefaults`, else the host's `defaultChatSettings`, except an orchestrated ticket chat, which takes its orchestrator's settings ([Fields and defaults](#fields-and-defaults)).
- The client that called the method selects the new chat: in the ticket Chats tab, in the bundle Chats tab, or, for feedback, in the Chats section of a main window chosen as [Feedback sheet](feedback.md#feedback-sheet) specifies, without opening a chat window. Chats started by `implement_ticket` and `implement_bundle` appear in the lists and are not opened.
- The screenshot of a feedback chat is the attachment of its first user message, sent to Claude as an `image` block. The link between the feedback chat and the ticket Claude creates is in [Feedback](feedback.md#resulting-ticket).

### Orchestrated ticket chats

The bundle chat started by Implement All or `implement_bundle` is the orchestrator of its bundle: it starts the ticket chats of its tickets, which run in parallel, each on its own branch in its own git worktree, waits for their reports and merges their branches ([Implement All](tickets.md#implement-all), [Claude workflow](workflow.md#bundles)).

- `chats.implementTicket` called by a `claude` actor whose chat is of kind `bundle`, for a ticket that this chat's bundle lists, creates an orchestrated ticket chat: its `orchestrator` is the calling chat ([Data model](data-model.md#chat-metadata-file)), and its first message is the rendered implement-bundle-ticket prompt. Every other call creates a ticket chat without `orchestrator`, whose first message is the rendered implement-ticket prompt.
- The orchestrated ticket chat takes the orchestrator's `settings`, its model, effort, permission mode and ultracode, as they are when `chats.implementTicket` is called. A change of the orchestrator's settings applies to the ticket chats it starts afterwards; the ticket chats already started keep theirs, and the composer of each changes them.
- The orchestrated ticket chat is in its ticket's `chats`. It is listed in the ticket's Chats tab, and in the bundle's Chats tab under its orchestrator ([Bundles section](tickets.md#bundles-section)). It is an implement chat ([Implement and feedback chats](#implement-and-feedback-chats)): it asks no questions until the user writes in it, and it asks its permission prompts in its own chat, where the user answers them.

#### Branch and worktree

Before it creates an orchestrated ticket chat, `chats.implementTicket` creates the chat's branch and worktree in the git repository of the project root. `<branch>` is `<bundle id>/<ticket id>`, with the bundle of the orchestrator chat, for example `HEPH-B18/HEPH-56`. `<worktree>` is `<data dir>/Worktrees/<project id>/<ticket id>` ([Data model](data-model.md#device-data-directory)). The steps, each a `git` command run in the project root ([Child processes](architecture.md#child-processes)):

1. `git worktree list --porcelain`. When `<worktree>` is one of the worktrees listed, `git worktree remove --force --force <worktree>`.
2. When the folder `<worktree>` still exists, the engine deletes it; then `git worktree prune`.
3. When `git show-ref --verify --quiet refs/heads/<branch>` exits with status 0, `git branch -D <branch>`.
4. `git worktree add -b <branch> <worktree> HEAD`: the branch starts from the commit of the project root's current branch, and the worktree checks it out.

- Steps 1 to 3 remove the worktree and the branch that an earlier chat of the ticket left: after a retry, a skipped ticket or a stopped bundle, the new chat starts on a fresh branch, and the work left on the old branch and in the old worktree is lost.
- When a step cannot be done, `chats.implementTicket` creates no chat and fails with `unavailable` and `Cannot create the branch and worktree of <TICKET-ID>: <reason>`:

| The step                                                                                         | `<reason>`                                                                                     |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| needs `git`, which is not available ([Child processes](architecture.md#child-processes), rule 5) | `git is not available`                                                                         |
| runs a command that cannot start                                                                 | `git cannot start: <error>`                                                                    |
| runs a command that does not end within 60 seconds                                               | `git did not finish within 60 seconds`                                                         |
| runs a command that exits with a non-zero status (except `git show-ref`)                         | the command's standard error, trimmed, or its standard output when the standard error is empty |
| cannot delete the remaining folder, or create the folder `Worktrees/<project id>/`               | `<path> cannot be deleted: <error>` or `<path> cannot be created: <error>`                     |

- In a project that is not a git repository, step 1 fails with git's `fatal: not a git repository` message.
- The engine prepares one worktree at a time, so that calls made together never run git in the same repository at once.

The commands, the deletion of a remaining folder, the timeout, the reasons and preparing one worktree at a time: Default chosen at design time.

#### Working directory

- An orchestrated ticket chat works in its worktree: its Claude process starts there at every launch, a resume included ([Launch](#launch)), its system prompt names the worktree and the branch as its working directory (`{{workingDirectory}}`, [Placeholders](#placeholders)), and `run_project` called from it runs the project's command there ([MCP](mcp.md#run_project)).
- The folder of the worktree it works in is `<worktree>` when the project root is the top folder of its git repository (the folder that holds `.git`). For a project whose root is a subfolder of the repository, a sub project for example, it is the same subfolder of `<worktree>`. Default chosen at design time.
- This holds at a launch while the chat's orchestrator chat exists, the chat is its ticket's newest orchestrated ticket chat (the last id of the ticket's `chats` whose chat has an `orchestrator`) and that folder exists. Otherwise the chat works in the project root, as the other chats do: after the bundle chat removed the worktree, or after a retry gave the worktree to a newer chat of the ticket. The condition on the orchestrator chat: Default chosen at design time.
- The ticket chat commits on its branch and does not push. The orchestrator rebases the branch onto the project root's current branch, fast-forwards that branch to it, and removes the worktree and the branch after its push ([Claude workflow](workflow.md#bundles)).
- The Run button and the chats that are not orchestrated ticket chats keep the project root.

#### Reports to the orchestrator

When the state of a chat with an `orchestrator` goes from `starting`, `running` or `waitingPermission` to `idle` or `error`, the engine sends the orchestrator chat one message, as the `system` actor, so its `sender` is the main device's name ([Engine API](engine-api.md#chatssend)):

| The turn                                                                                        | Message                                                                      |
| ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| was stopped with Stop                                                                           | `Chat of <TICKET-ID> was stopped.`                                           |
| left the chat in state `error`, or its last `result` has `isError` without being a stopped turn | `Chat of <TICKET-ID> ended in error: <error message>`                        |
| ended otherwise                                                                                 | `Chat of <TICKET-ID> finished its turn:`, a blank line, then the final reply |

- `<TICKET-ID>` is the ticket chat's `ticket`. The final reply is the text of the turn's last `assistant` item without `parentToolUseID`, empty when the turn has none. The error message is the text of the turn's last `system` item of level `error` when there is one, otherwise the `summary` of the turn's last `result`.
- A turn that ends in `waitingAnswers`, on `ask_questions`, sends nothing: the orchestrator keeps waiting, and the turn that follows the answers reports.
- Nothing is sent while the engine stops, or when the orchestrator chat no longer exists or is being deleted; `engine.log` records the skipped message.
- The orchestrator receives the message in any state ([Chat states](#chat-states)): during one of its turns, the CLI runs it after that turn. A closed orchestrator chat is reopened by the message ([Closing a chat](#closing-a-chat)).
- The turns of an orchestrated ticket chat post no "finished" notification, and a bundle chat posts one only for a turn that ends while none of the ticket chats it orchestrates is working; their "needs you" notifications are posted as for every chat ([Notifications](#notifications)).

The other rules of this section are Default chosen at design time, except the message texts, the settings taken from the orchestrator, the branch, the worktree and its folder, the working directory, `run_project` in the worktree, the removal of an earlier branch and worktree, and the failure when they cannot be created, which the user chose.

### Feedback message

`chats.createFeedback` composes the text of the feedback chat's first user message from the comment and the FeedbackContext ([Feedback](feedback.md#feedback-context)). Default chosen at design time.

```
<comment>

Feedback context:
- App: HephaestusV2 v<appVersion>(<build>)
- Device: <device>
- Project: <project>
- Section: <section>
- Item: <item>
- Screenshot: <screenshot path>
```

| Placeholder         | Value                                                                                                                                                 |
| ------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `<comment>`         | the comment, trimmed; `(no comment)` when it is empty                                                                                                 |
| `<device>`          | `deviceName`, or `<deviceName>, connected to <mainDeviceName>` when the two differ                                                                    |
| `<project>`         | `<project name> (<projectID>)`, the identifier standing for the name of a project that is not loaded; the line is left out when `projectID` is absent |
| `<section>`         | `section`; the line is left out when it is absent                                                                                                     |
| `<item>`            | `item`; the line is left out when it is absent                                                                                                        |
| `<screenshot path>` | absolute path, on the main device, of the stored screenshot                                                                                           |

- Lines are separated by `\n`; the text does not end with a line break. The `user` item stores this text.
- The screenshot is the message's only attachment and is sent as an `image` block; its path is in the text, so Claude can attach it to the ticket.

Example, for feedback sent from a remote window of the MacBook showing the device Studio:

```
The lock icon disappears when I drag a locked tab to another position.

Feedback context:
- App: HephaestusV2 v0.4.0(57)
- Device: MacBook, connected to Studio
- Project: Hephaestus V2 (HEPH)
- Section: web
- Item: https://github.com/
- Screenshot: /Users/example/Developer/HephaestusV2/.hephaestus/chats/3F2504E0-4F89-11D3-9A0C-0305E82C3301/1B4E28BA-2FA1-11D2-883F-0016D3CCA427-screenshot.png
```

## Haiku naming

A Haiku call names tickets created without a title, bundles created without a title, and every chat after its first user message. A summary call, the same command with another prompt, writes the detail of the chat notifications ([Summary call](#summary-call)).

### Command

```
<CLI path> -p --model haiku --output-format json --no-session-persistence --tools "" --strict-mcp-config --mcp-config '{"mcpServers":{}}' --system-prompt <naming prompt> <input>
```

- The command is shown with shell quoting; the engine passes each argument as one argv element, without a shell.
- `<naming prompt>` is the resource naming-ticket, naming-bundle or naming-chat, or notification-summary for a summary call, unchanged. `<input>` is built as below and never starts with `-`.
- Working directory: `<data dir>/Runtime/claude-cwd/`, an empty folder created when missing ([Architecture](architecture.md#child-processes)); environment: [Environment](#environment). Default chosen at design time.
- Timeout 30 s: SIGTERM, then SIGKILL 2 s later ([Signals](#signals)); the provisional title stays.
- At most 2 naming calls run at once; the others wait in first-in first-out order. Default chosen at design time.
- Failures and timeouts are written to `engine.log`.

### Inputs

| Subject | `<input>`                                                                              | Maximum title length | Provisional title                       |
| ------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------- | --------------------------------------- |
| ticket  | `Ticket description:` + `\n` + the description                                         | 70                   | [Tickets](tickets.md#titles-and-naming) |
| bundle  | `Tickets:` + `\n` + one line `<ticket id>: <ticket title>` per ticket, in bundle order | 60                   | [Tickets](tickets.md#bundle-rules)      |
| chat    | `First message:` + `\n` + the text of the chat's first `user` item that has text       | 60                   | `New Chat`                              |

- The first line of each `<input>` (the label) and the part after it, cut at 4,000 characters: Default chosen at design time.
- When the first user message of a chat has no text, the chat is named from the first later user message that has text.

### Output cleanup

1. Parse stdout as one JSON object. A parse failure, a non-zero exit code or `is_error` true keeps the provisional title.
2. Take `result`, trim it, and keep its first line.
3. Remove a leading `Title:`, `Ticket title:`, `Bundle title:` or `Chat title:` (any letter case) and trim. Default chosen at design time.
4. Remove surrounding quote characters (`"`, `'`, `“`, `”`, `‘`, `’`, `«`, `»`, `` ` ``) from both ends, repeatedly, and trim.
5. Remove trailing periods and trim. Steps 4 and 5 run again, in this order, until neither changes the text: `` `Fix it`. `` and `"Fix it."` both give `Fix it`. Default chosen at design time.
6. When the text is longer than the maximum, cut it at the last space at or before the maximum (at the maximum when there is no space) and trim. Default chosen at design time.
7. An empty text keeps the provisional title.

### Applying the title

- The Haiku title replaces the title only when the current title still equals the provisional title, so a title set meanwhile by the user or by Claude wins.
- The update emits `ticket.updated`, `bundle.updated` or `chat.updated` with origin `system`. Chat windows follow the chat title.
- Naming is not retried, after a failure or after an app restart.

### Summary call

A summary call writes the detail of a chat notification ([Notifications](#notifications)). It runs the [command](#command) with the prompt notification-summary, in the same working directory and environment. After 30 s it receives SIGTERM, then SIGKILL 2 s later ([Signals](#signals)), and the notification takes the fallback detail.

| Event                        | `<input>`                                                                                                                        |
| ---------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| finished                     | `Claude's reply:` + `\n` + Claude's last reply of the turn                                                                       |
| needs you, permission        | `Permission request:` + `\n` + `Tool: <tool name>` + `\n` + `Input: <input>`, the input as JSON without indentation, keys sorted |
| needs you, question set      | `Questions:` + `\n` + one line `<label>: <question>` per question, in label order                                                |
| needs you, symbol request    | `Symbol request:` + `\n` + the question                                                                                          |
| needs you, color request     | `Color request:` + `\n` + the question                                                                                           |
| needs you, sound request     | `Sound request:` + `\n` + the question                                                                                           |
| needs you, shortcut request  | `Shortcut request:` + `\n` + the question                                                                                        |
| needs you, font request      | `Font request:` + `\n` + the question                                                                                            |
| needs you, animation request | `Animation request:` + `\n` + the question                                                                                       |

- The part after the label is cut at 4,000 characters, as for naming.
- At most 2 summary calls run at once, besides the naming calls; the others wait in first-in first-out order. While the Claude CLI is unavailable the call fails at once. Failures and timeouts are written to `engine.log`.
- Output cleanup:
  1. Parse stdout as one JSON object. A parse failure, a non-zero exit code or `is_error` true gives the fallback detail.
  2. Take `result`, trim it, and replace each run of white space, line breaks included, with one space.
  3. Remove a leading `Summary:` (any letter case) and trim.
  4. Remove surrounding quote characters (those of [Output cleanup](#output-cleanup)) from both ends, repeatedly, and trim.
  5. When the text is longer than 150 characters, cut it at the last space at or before the maximum, 150 (at the maximum when there is no space), and trim, as naming step 6 does.
  6. An empty text gives the fallback detail.

The inputs, the queue and the cleanup are Default chosen at design time; the 150-character limit and the 30 s timeout come from the user.

## Comment answers

A comment answer is a one-shot call of the Claude CLI that answers a ticket comment that mentions Claude, without a chat, as Haiku naming runs: no session is kept, and the call adds no chat, no transcript and no item to the Chats section or to the ticket's Chats tab. The engine of the main device starts it when `tickets.addComment` stores a new comment of a user that mentions Claude, and again for each Retry ([Mentioning Claude](tickets.md#mentioning-claude), [Comment answers](tickets.md#comment-answers)).

### Answer command

```
<CLI path> -p --output-format json --no-session-persistence --model <model> --effort <effort> --permission-mode dontAsk --tools Read,Grep,Glob --allowedTools <answer tools> --strict-mcp-config --mcp-config <data dir>/Runtime/mcp/answer-<answer id>.json --append-system-prompt <rendered comment-answer prompt> <input>
```

- The command is shown with shell quoting; the engine passes each argument as one argv element, without a shell.
- `<model>` and `<effort>` are the `model` and the `effort` of the project's `chatDefaults`, else of the host's `defaultChatSettings` ([Chat settings](data-model.md#chat-settings)); their `permissionMode` and `ultracode` are not used.
- `--tools Read,Grep,Glob` leaves Claude the read-only file tools of the CLI and no other built-in tool. `--permission-mode dontAsk` refuses, without asking, every tool that `--allowedTools` does not list.
- `<answer tools>` is this single argument: `Read,Grep,Glob,mcp__hephaestus__list_projects,mcp__hephaestus__get_project,mcp__hephaestus__list_tickets,mcp__hephaestus__get_ticket,mcp__hephaestus__update_ticket,mcp__hephaestus__set_ticket_status,mcp__hephaestus__list_bundles,mcp__hephaestus__get_bundle,mcp__hephaestus__search,mcp__hephaestus__list_docs,mcp__hephaestus__list_web_tabs,mcp__hephaestus__list_skills,mcp__hephaestus__get_skill,mcp__hephaestus__list_chats,mcp__hephaestus__get_chat`: the tools that read HephaestusV2 data, plus `update_ticket` and `set_ticket_status`.
- `--strict-mcp-config` keeps every other MCP server away. `<answer id>` is a new UUID for each call. Before the call the engine writes the MCP configuration file `Runtime/mcp/answer-<answer id>.json` with file mode 0600, in the format of the [MCP configuration file](#mcp-configuration-file) of chats, with a new answer token; the MCP server maps the token to the actor `claude` with the chatID `answer` and limits it to the tools above and to the comment's ticket ([MCP](mcp.md#authentication)). When the call ends, whatever the reason, the token is revoked and the file is deleted.
- `<rendered comment-answer prompt>` is the resource comment-answer rendered for the comment's ticket ([Prompt: comment-answer](#prompt-comment-answer)). `<input>` is built as [Answer input](#answer-input) specifies and starts with `Ticket:`, never with `-`.
- Working directory: the root of the ticket's project. Environment: [Environment](#environment), without the hook token: the call has no hook.
- The number of calls running at once is not limited. A call starts once the Claude CLI is available; while the status is checking, it waits for the computation to end, at most 20 seconds, as the chat methods do ([Version and status](#version-and-status)).
- A call still running 5 minutes after its start receives SIGTERM, then SIGKILL 5 s later ([Signals](#signals)). Deleting the comment or its ticket, or removing the ticket's project, ends its call the same way at once. A call is registered with the engine's helper processes, so quitting the app ends it as [Shutdown](architecture.md#shutdown) specifies.

The flags, the answer tools and the 5 s before SIGKILL are Default chosen at design time; the 5-minute timeout, the model and the effort come from the user.

### Answer input

`<input>` is the line `Ticket:`, the ticket rendered as `{{ticket}}` ([Rendered blocks](#rendered-blocks)), every chat of the ticket that has metadata listed under `Chats:`, then a blank line, the line `Comment to answer:` and the comment rendered as the comments of `{{ticket}}` are, its attachments included:

```
Ticket:
ID: HEPH-12
Title: Locked web tabs close with Command-W
…
Comments:
- 2026-10-31T12:10:00Z by user:
  @Claude why does this ticket depend on HEPH-9?
Chats: none

Comment to answer:
- 2026-10-31T12:10:00Z by user:
  @Claude why does this ticket depend on HEPH-9?
```

The ticket is rendered as it is when the call starts, so a Retry gets the ticket as it is then. Its attachment paths, and those of the comment, are absolute paths on the main device, which Claude reads with its file tools. The 30,000-character cut of `{{ticket}}` applies to the ticket part only. Default chosen at design time.

### Answer output

When the process exits, the engine reads its standard output as one JSON object:

1. When the output is a JSON object whose `total_cost_usd` is a number of at least 0, the cost is stored in the ticket's project, whatever the outcome, as [Comment answers](costs.md#comment-answers) specifies.
2. The call succeeded when the process exited with status 0, the output is a JSON object, its `is_error` is not true and its `result` is a string that is not empty after trimming.
3. On success, the engine adds the trimmed `result` as a new comment of the ticket with `tickets.addComment` and the actor `claude` with the chatID `answer`, so its author is Claude and it starts no answer. The answer state of the mentioning comment is cleared in the same `ticket.updated`.
4. Otherwise the call failed: the answer state of the comment becomes `failed` and the engine writes `comment answer for <ticket id> failed: <reason>` to `engine.log` with the level WARN, `<reason>` being `timeout`, `the Claude CLI is not available`, `could not start: <error>`, `exit code <n>` (128 + the signal number for a process ended by a signal), `the output is not a JSON object`, `is_error is true`, `the result is empty` or `the answer cannot be posted: <message>`, when `tickets.addComment` fails.
5. When the comment or its ticket no longer exists, nothing is posted and no state is kept; a cost reported by the call is still stored.

The changes Claude made to the ticket with `update_ticket` and `set_ticket_status` during the call stay, whatever the outcome. The log texts are Default chosen at design time.

## Notifications

The engine reports two kinds of chat events, which the Macs showing the chat's device turn into macOS notifications ([UI › Status bar](ui.md#status-bar)); the [Notifications](ui.md#notifications) tab of each Mac's Settings turns them off on that Mac.

| Kind      | API value  | Event                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| --------- | ---------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| needs you | `needsYou` | The chat's state becomes `waitingPermission` or `waitingAnswers` (a question set, a symbol request, a color request, a sound request, a shortcut request, a font request or an animation request) from another state. |
| finished  | `finished` | A turn ends ([Turn tracking](#turn-tracking)) and the chat's state becomes `idle`.                                                                                                                                    |

- The `error` state posts nothing ([Chat states](#chat-states)). A turn that ends in `waitingAnswers` posts only the "needs you" notification.
- No "finished" notification for a turn stopped with Stop ([Stopping a turn](#stopping-a-turn)), for a turn of an orchestrated ticket chat ([Orchestrated ticket chats](#orchestrated-ticket-chats)), or for a turn of a bundle chat that ends while one of the ticket chats it orchestrates, the chats whose `orchestrator` is that bundle chat, is working ([Chat states](#chat-states)). A bundle chat thus posts "finished" when its turn ends and none of its ticket chats is working. These chats post their "needs you" notifications as every chat.
- Only a change from a state that `chat.updated` already reported counts. The states the engine computes when it starts, or when it replays the transcripts of a project loaded later ([Data model](data-model.md#loading)), post nothing, so a restart does not post again for the chats that were already waiting.

### Title and detail

- Title: `<project identifier> · <chat title>`, for example `HEPH · Status history for tickets`. Each Mac builds it from the chat's metadata when it posts.
- Detail: a summary of at most 150 characters written by the Haiku model ([Summary call](#summary-call)) from:
